---

## 4. How the cast sounds in English

One sentence per principal, derived from the EN correlates lines in section 1 only. These are the DEFAULT
targets; every register break recorded in section 1 overrides the default for the length of the break.
Two project-wide floors: no character except one nameless video presenter goes above "damn"/"crap", and
honorifics stay on as suffixes (STYLE.md), so English formality must be carried by DICTION, not by dropping
or adding a suffix.

- **古郡なつみ** — soft, plain, medium contractions ("I'm", "don't", never "gonna"), short sentences and fragments, trailing ellipses, no slang and no jargon, and ZERO profanity in 39 files; keep the repeated "I", because deleting it to smooth the prose deletes her.
- **新村春花** — clipped and boyish, HIGH contractions ("gonna", "c'mon", "yeah"), short imperative-heavy sentences, low formality even to police, food-and-enthusiasm vocabulary, and a hard ceiling of "damn"/"crap" that she reaches often and never passes.
- **五島絵梨奈** — bright polite schoolgirl on top of a precise technical vocabulary underneath, medium-high contractions but always courteous ("sir", "boss"), medium explanatory sentences that go numbered when she reasons, zero profanity — and the formal register must be able to sound MENACING without one impolite word.
- **古郡良治** — dry ordinary-salaryman warmth, medium contractions in speech and none at all in his letter, short sentences in both, a ceiling of "damn", and never a word about what he felt.
- **古郡茜** — warm-informal and domestic, medium contractions, short sentences, zero profanity, a working mother in her early forties and NOT an old woman, and the same level competent voice whether she is cooking or assigning a murder.
- **新村美冬** — a forty-year-old over-performing youth: exclamation marks everywhere and elongated vowels ("soooo", "reeeally") in register 1 with high contractions, dropping to very short, level, medium-contraction sentences with no exclamation marks at all in register 2.
- **五島桃子** — very short, blunt, high-contraction lines to her sister and medium-length polished ones to a guest; rough words with a protective errand inside them, unpleasant without ever being a villain, and profanity only when somebody insults her sister.
- **伊勢大二郎** — four English speeds: no contractions in the briefing, none in the rant, very short barks in command, fragmentary mumbling when gentle; a ceiling of "damn"; and real police procedure against absurd hobby vocabulary, landing him as a comic figure and never a threat.
- **新村栄一郎** — formal even when broken: low contractions, short sentences that are almost all apology, self-deprecation or a question about someone else's comfort, zero profanity, and "become a family" fixed as the same three words every time.
- **新村茅萱** — low contractions, short unhurried sentences, never rude and never coarse, and the cruelty entirely in the content; one fixed English rendering of the 「ん……」 opener used on every spoken line and on none of her narration.
- **新村夏菜** — very short, high-contraction, zero-formality eleven-year-old, over-familiar to everyone; adult and internet words she has picked up rather than understood, and the shock is that a child is saying them.
- **新村幸太郎** — medium contractions, short plain sentences, low-warm formality, zero profanity, and the antagonist version is the same voice with the warmth removed: never louder, never coarser, demands stated as offers.
- **新村エリカ** — medium contractions and short sentences in her own voice with no verbal habits at all, whatever the target's diction is inside an impression, and profanity only ever inside one.
- **新村サクラ** — low contractions, short warm sentences, zero profanity, no tic and no catchphrase of any kind, every opening line a question about the listener, and gentle without being saccharine.
- **城崎健吾** — medium-high contractions and an exclamation mark on most lines when the loud register is on, and low contractions with short level sentences when it is off; the switch is the character, so English must be able to run him silent for a whole scene.
- **navigator / 立木三日** — hotel-concierge English applied to mass murder: zero contractions, long nominalised sentences, maximal unvarying formality, and identical wording across every menu file.
- **糸姫** — zero contractions, very short sentences, the highest formality of any human in the project, a servant child's vocabulary with no abstract nouns, and not one rough form in eight hundred years.
- **皇子** — zero contractions, medium one-clause-per-line sentences, medium-archaic without being stiff, court-educated but plain, no tic at all, and the flattest voice in the project after 春花's autobiography mode.
- **新村桔梗** — zero contractions, essayistic reasoning-first sentences that set out a principle and then apply it, high formality throughout, and one deliberately silly repeated joke with a different elongation each time.
- **藤吉郎** — medium-high contractions, short driven question-heavy sentences, zero profanity in 500 cells, period-plain with peasant edges and a rising administrative vocabulary when he reasons; keep every "I".
- **内府** — zero contractions, medium-to-long measured narration and very short speech, the highest formality outside the menu voice, political and military vocabulary, and no warmth and no villainy: an administrator.
- **篠崎ハジメ** — no contractions, short clipped bureaucratic-military sentences, very high formality, zero profanity, and under pressure only the volume moves.
- **五島桃子's and 伊勢's narration, and 城崎's, 幸太郎's, 良治's and 美冬's** — all six drop their surface tics entirely when they hold the first person: plain past, short declaratives, one thought per cell, no self-pity. The comic or loud surface belongs to the dialogue only, and English must keep that split visible.
- **後白河糸織** — high contractions, short fast zero-formality sentences, plain physical vocabulary, and NOT a British or American regional dialect; the Osaka register is an owner-blocking decision (Q611).
- **the 核 / 女神様** — zero contractions, very short and often ungrammatical fragments, no formality of any kind, a pre-school vocabulary with not one abstract noun, and nine turns that are blank cells rather than lines.

# PART B — CONVENTIONS, DECISIONS, HAZARDS
## 1. Typographic devices and the rendering rule for each

Devices are ordered roughly by how often a translator will hit them. Each entry: what it is, how often,
the rule, the query ids. "DECISION NEEDED" means the queries ask and never answer.

### 1.1 「」 speech → straight double quotes
The default. Every cell of ordinary audible speech. Rule (STYLE.md): 「」 → "...". A JP line that is only
「」 with a line break inside keeps the break. The important thing is that 「」 is **not** a universal
speech marker in this script — six other systems below carry speech too, and one file (00000EAF) makes
「」 mean "this person can physically speak" rather than "this is dialogue" (Q254).
Ids: Q036, Q254, Q134.

### 1.2 『』 → single quotes
~150+ occurrences across the project. Five distinct jobs, all rendered the same way:
- quoted written documents read aloud (Q504, Q531 a 130-year-old document, Q534 a 大魔女's memo, Q916 the
  founder quoted 800 years later, Q951 a circulated notice);
- a named concept held at arm's length: 『恵み』 ~20 times and never defined (Q382), 『信頼できる人』 five
  times and never identified (Q118), 『起』『承』『転』『結』 ~40 times as a reasoning frame (Q166),
  『真相の「根」』 (Q468), 『元木町がなくなる』 a prophecy repeated four times (Q517);
- speech that is not being spoken: 『あれ』 four times for menarche (Q357), specimen phrases nobody is
  saying 『うそ！？』『ほんとに！？』 (Q293), what the narrator would feel if the body were a stranger's (Q068);
- book titles and invented names: 『のろいのせかい』 a children's book written all in hiragana (Q090),
  『ルーシー人形』 (Q241), 『死月妖花』 (Q495), 『おひめの歌』 (Q789), 『オカルト雑誌M』 (Q947);
- the ancestral transmission, which is printed six times in three typographic systems (Q567, Q619, Q841).
Rule: 『』 → '...' everywhere, and let register carry the rest — the 450-year-old book is plainer and
shorter than the narration around it, so do not gild it (Q385, high). Where a document is quoted
*differently* in two files, keep the two treatments distinct rather than harmonising them (Q504, medium).
Ids: Q068, Q090, Q118, Q166, Q241, Q293, Q357, Q382, Q385, Q468, Q495, Q503, Q504, Q517, Q531, Q534,
Q560, Q567, Q619, Q789, Q841, Q916, Q947, Q951, Q968, Q970.

### 1.3 The 「――」 line-initial prefix — FIVE different jobs
The single most dangerous device in the script. A dash at the head of a cell, inside or outside quotes.
It has five separate meanings and the script never disambiguates them:

1. **Speaker channel.** In a file with no narrator, 「――」 marks one speaker's lines and 「」 the other's,
   with no names anywhere. Whole files work this way: 000003A8/000003AE/000003B4/000003BC (Q047),
   000020BB two soldiers (Q835), 00002078 two hospital staff with *no register difference at all* (Q836),
   00002084 an izakaya master against two customers (Q844), 00001BA2 and 00001BA8 where the 「――」 side is a
   dead woman in one file and a crowd in the other (Q801), 0000243F where the narrator's own lines are 「」
   and every other resident is a bare 「――」 (Q928), 00001D34 (Q793).
   Rule: keep 「――」 as a literal line-initial marker (Q047, high; Q835/Q836/Q844 high).
2. **Static dropout mid-word.** 「聞こえ――ますが」, 「現地到着――まで30分を見込んで――ください」 — a radio
   breaking up. Rule: keep them as dropouts and break the English in the same places, mid-word where the JP
   breaks mid-word (Q064, high).
3. **Voices from off-screen / unhearable voices.** Speakers not present when they speak (Q169), 元木町 voices
   heard from a helicopter (Q264), voices out of the trees (Q623), a returning sense rather than a voice —
   「――いる。」「――ここよりもずっと高いところに。」 (Q589), a rival's private thoughts inside another
   narrator's first person (Q265). Rule: keep the dash, attribute nothing, and do **not** add "I imagined"
   or "I felt" or "it said" (Q264 medium, Q589 medium, Q623 high, Q169 medium).
4. **The narrator's own transmitted thought.** At 0000151C:8:174-294 the dash prefixes the NARRATOR's
   telepathic output and ordinary 「」 holds the other party's replies — the inversion of job 1.
   Rule: keep the inversion; it is the file's way of saying her thoughts are leaving her head (Q618, medium).
5. **An ordinary audible secondary speaker.** From 00001818 onward the dash marks a perfectly audible person
   who is simply not the focus: the second of two classmates, funeral mourners, the coffee-house master,
   the clinic doctor, the studio host. **Q709 is owner-blocking (high).** A documentary uses it for the
   programme's own narration returning after a quoted expert (Q690). Press conferences use it for
   reporters' questions against 「」 for the spokesman (Q656). Broadcasts use it for the live report
   (Q359, Q414).
Additional: one cell can hold a 「――」 line and a 「」 line joined by ⏎ (Q808, Q816, Q845) — keep both
markers inside the one cell.
Ids: Q047, Q064, Q169, Q264, Q265, Q359, Q414, Q589, Q618, Q623, Q656, Q690, Q709 (owner-blocking),
Q793, Q801, Q808, Q816, Q835, Q836, Q844, Q845, Q897, Q928, Q937.

### 1.4 A cell containing only 「――」
Four instances, all at a moment a character cannot or will not answer: 00000F0D:62:129-134 at a suicide
attempt (Q358), 0000127A:8:160 with a hand over her mouth (Q509), 000013CD:8:190 pinned and bled on
(Q556), 0000139D:8:72-74 three consecutive such cells followed by 「男なのか女なのか……判別できない」 (Q550).
Rule: dashes only, per STYLE (Q509 suggests four dashes for 「――――」, medium). Keep the cell count, add
nothing, and do not let the surrounding narration supply a pronoun (Q550, high). 00001505:8:190 must be
rendered identically to whatever 0000127A gets (Q556, high).

### 1.5 Empty 「」 cells
~45 instances. A pair of quote marks with nothing inside, standing for a turn that does not come.
- 0000050E:11:136/138/184 — a dead phone twice, then a person who has stopped answering (Q062).
- 00000E7B:88:84 — placed where a dying man's next line would be (Q237).
- 00000EF6 — ~36 of them, the comatose 春花's turn in a one-sided conversation, and separately 「…………」
  cells for something the narrator believes she nearly heard. **The two are different and must stay
  different in EN** (Q320, high).
- 00000F24:187:33 and 187:35 — two, one line apart, where a woman dies mid-sentence (Q389).
Rule: preserve the empty quoted cell exactly; never insert an ellipsis; flag every one so no cleanup pass
deletes it (Q062 high, Q237 high, Q320 high, Q389 high).

### 1.6 Straight double quotes in the SOURCE
Confirmed meaning (PROGRESS chunk 07, Q134/Q036): **text that nobody present is speaking** — not "phone
message". ~20 sites. Text messages (Q036, Q298, Q471 with a kaomoji inside, Q795, Q902 a 22-cell exchange
containing a murder confession, Q398), station and drill announcements (Q134), a web-search no-results
line (Q161), a film title (Q183), English words glossed in the next cell (Q231), TV news interleaved into
a death scene (Q238), a mute narrator's every utterance for 2,241 lines (Q254), a 400-year-old document
(Q503), a bracketed placeholder inside a reconstruction (Q579), a photograph message rendered as ten
full-width spaces inside the quotes (Q299).
Rule: **keep straight double quotes in EN too** (Q298, Q398, Q503, Q795 all say so). Never quotation-mark
the mute narrator into speaking (Q254, high). Keep (^-^) exactly as printed (Q471). Keep the ten spaces
(Q299). Q036 (high) still wants one fixed wrapper decision covering messages, announcements and broadcasts
before any of those files is translated — widen the rule to cover announcements (Q134, high).
**Collision hazard:** 「」 also becomes "..." in EN, so the JP distinction between 「」 and "" vanishes
unless a second marker is chosen. This is unresolved. **DECISION NEEDED** — best guess from Q036/Q071/Q079:
give written documents one wrapper and phone messages another; confidence high that a decision is needed,
no wording proposed.

### 1.7 Bare unquoted cells — the unmarked-format problem
The script has at least **five** text formats that look like narration and are not, and they are never
labelled at the point of use:
- **Letters.** 00000564:11:97-120 (24 cells, 古郡良治's letter) (Q071); 0000062C:11:61-66 a handwritten
  letter and 11:70-76 a phone message **set in the same unmarked format** (Q079); 0000082F:27:32-38 a
  farewell letter; 000014D7:8:353-357 a note whose last cell is only the signature (Q593).
- **Memos and reports.** 0000136D:8:27-51, ~25 cells of です・ます inside a plain-form first person, framed
  only afterwards (Q542); 000013FD:8:241-253 a teaching recalled from memory with no marks at all (Q560);
  00001355:8:45-48 a 大魔女's memo in 『』 with a two-line signature (Q534).
- **Scripture and documents.** 00001247:8:75-120, ~45 cells of scripture as bare narration, four cells
  before a three-line passage of the same kind set in straight quotes (Q503, Q504).
- **Registry and 戸籍 extracts.** 00000A94:11:35 and 00000AAC:11:16 with kanji numerals and ⏎ inside one
  cell (Q163); 00000EF6:26:140-152 two 戸籍謄本 extracts with field labels and full-width column spacing
  (Q324).
- **Ceremonial speech.** 000014BC:8:4-11, seven cells of an 入学式 address as bare narration, labelled only
  at 8:11 (Q586); 00002090 and 00002096, two whole files that are one political speech each with no
  narrator and no frame (Q833).
- **Voices and one-liners.** 00000FCF five row-blocks of a dead woman's voice on the wind (Q428);
  00001039 an internet presenter's whole patter (Q413); 000021C9:12:0 and 00001FF5:12:0 single unquoted
  cells with no terminal stop (Q838, Q780); 0000243F:11:106-113 a four-item list whose fourth entry is a
  bare cell reading 「そして」 (Q929); 00002444:32:341 (Q941).
- **Chat logs.** 000020FC: every cell is `handle⏎message`, and six cells hold three handles and three
  messages joined by ⏎⏎ (Q831). 00001A65: several complete board posts with timestamps and handles joined
  inside ONE cell by ⏎ (Q733).
Rule: keep every one of these unquoted and unindented exactly as printed, keep the line breaks, keep the
signature alone on its own line, and let the register shift be the only signal — the JP gives no other
(Q542 high, Q560 medium, Q586 medium, Q593 medium, Q428 high, Q833 high, Q838 high, Q780 high, Q831 high,
Q733 high, Q324 high, Q413 medium, Q573 medium).
Open: Q071 and Q079 (both high) want ONE wrapper for written documents and ONE for phone messages, decided
before 00000564 and 0000062C are translated. **DECISION NEEDED**, best guess: two distinct wrappers,
confidence high that it must be settled, no wording chosen.

### 1.8 The indented bare cell (two leading full-width spaces)
A narrower case of 1.7, used at least eight times across chunks 08-10 for five different sources: a notice
board quoting itself, a character's own voice breaking out of 「」, a flat answer to a question
(000009FE:15:0, 23:0, 31:0-1; 00000A62:15:0; 00000AC4:15:0).
Rule: keep the two full-width leading spaces, add nothing, never tag a speaker (Q158, high).

### 1.9 The one-cell reveal row-block
A whole row-block ([15], [23], [12], [46] and others) containing ONE cell, holding the object or completion
of a sentence begun in the previous block, so the engine's page break lands on the punch. Used at least
**thirteen** times: 00000E4E3:15:0 「今まで6人殺した。」 and 23:0 「お前も今日死ぬ」 (Q061); five times in
chunk 09 (Q179); 00000E01:15:0 「おいで」 (Q215); 00000EF6:46:0 「死ね」 with no speaker and no context
(Q321); 0000110D:12:0 and 20:0 「あっちの方にいるよ」 replayed verbatim ten years later (Q456);
000014EE:12:0 (Q621); 00001C5D:15:0 (Q806); 000021C9:12:0 (Q838); 0000235A:12:0 「血まみれだった」 (Q899);
00001FF5:12:0 (Q780); 0000156A twice, both completing a sentence about the same name (Q652).
Rule: **keep the split, and order the English clause so the withheld element still lands last in the second
cell** (Q179, high; Q028 is the general "punch lands alone, key noun last" rule). Keep the cell bare and
unquoted where the JP is bare, keep it quoted where the JP quotes it — 000006E0 block [12] is the same
engine device with 「」 and an attributable speaker, and the brackets stay there and stay absent at
000004E3 (Q061, high). Never merge into the preceding cell (Q899, high). Keep the break exactly where the
JP puts it (Q652, high).

### 1.10 Ruby-residue spacing
Stray half-width or full-width spaces around a kanji — 「 猿 」, 「 掟 」, 「 絆 」, 「 躯 」, 「 唆 」 —
left behind when the ruby (furigana) was stripped from the dump. **~60 instances**, clustering hard on
kanji a Japanese reader might not be able to read: はらわた for intestines, おきて for a village law,
すくも for a scale, 蝕 む, 椿, 出 奔, 礎, 踵, 李, 庇, 囁, 慄, 懐, 妾, 厠, 曙, 兎 狐 猪 狸 in one list.
Also inside proper nouns: 「南 崎しのり」 (Q268), 「宝 町 駅」 (Q476), 「新村 晃」 (Q595), 『金崎 勉』 (Q877).
And inside a Latin-script gloss: 「H o w」 (Q467).
Rule: **strip the spacing and translate normally.** The ruby was the reading; if the reading matters to the
scene, use a [TN:] instead (Q624 high, Q851 high, Q852 high, Q787 high, Q788 high, Q566 high, Q975 high,
Q323 low, Q671 low, Q209 low). Keep the list for a possible ruby-restoration pass later (Q566).
Note the one place the spacing is diagnostic: 000021BD:21:39-43 prints the ancestral instruction for the
sixth time and is the ONLY printing with no ruby residue — evidence that block [21] is a different
document (Q841, cross-refs owner-blocking Q827).
Ids: Q010, Q148, Q209, Q268, Q323, Q375, Q431, Q467, Q476, Q535, Q566, Q576, Q595, Q624, Q671, Q787,
Q788, Q841, Q851, Q852, Q877, Q908, Q953, Q975.

### 1.11 ⏎ (in-cell line break)
Four jobs:
1. **Two or more speakers joined in one cell.** ~20 cells. Two people silent 「…………」⏎「…………」, two people
   in unison 「せーの！」⏎「せーの！」 / 「却下！」⏎「却下！」 / 「ふむふむ」⏎「ふむふむ」, two bandits
   laughing, two people shouting at one body 「お母さん！」⏎「サクラ！」, a cut-off line plus the
   interrupter, five 「――はーい！」 in one cell, a reporter and a studio host reacting at once across the
   two speaker channels.
   Rule: keep the line break and keep every set of quotes inside the one cell; consider a shared
   attribution in the surrounding narration (Q044 high, Q511 high, Q554 high, Q620 medium, Q794 med,
   Q808 high, Q816 med, Q845 high, Q898 medium, Q904 medium).
2. **Layout.** 「計⏎画⏎通⏎り」 one character per line filling the screen vertically (Q076); a seven-item
   legend with definitions built from leading full-width spaces and blank ⏎ lines (Q721, owner-blocking);
   a UI label padded with ⏎ (Q721); the 29-dish list packed several per cell with ⏎ after being printed
   once as one item per cell (Q260); register entries with kanji numerals (Q163); generation tables
   (Q384, owner-blocking).
   Rule: reproduce the shape approximately and record in NOTES-TL.md that exact alignment is impossible
   (Q721, high). Reproduce both layouts of the dish list and keep the item order identical (Q260, medium).
3. **Chat and board posts.** See 1.7 (Q733, Q831).
4. **Two full sentences joined in a narration cell** where the file's other 3,000 cells hold one each —
   0000156A:58:13 and 76:33. Rule: keep the break, do not merge or split (Q662, low).

### 1.12 Row-blocks printed out of scene order
**A whole-file hazard, not a line-level one.** The engine prints row-blocks in ascending block number and
several authors' files are deliberately written out of order. Confirmed cases:
- 000006F9 — [11] happens AFTER [29], with [17] between (Q096).
- 0000088B — [16] and [22] are two versions of one arrival differing by whether a sleeping child is in
  the car; [28] is the choice that produces them and [35] precedes both (Q120).
- 00000EAF — thirteen blocks in almost exactly reverse story order; the first printed is the last event,
  ~15 in-story years later (Q253).
- 00000EDF — 21 blocks shuffled; the two earliest events are printed last (Q286; story order recorded in
  the row, medium-high).
- 00000EF6 — fourteen blocks in exactly reverse order except one three-block run that goes forward inside
  the reversal (Q317).
- 00000F0D — first twelve chronological, last two printed are not; the central revelation is printed last
  (Q351).
- 00000F24 — sixteen blocks ascending and that is not story order; chronological order recorded (Q380).
- 0000156A — nineteen blocks; the file opens near its own end and prints its first two blocks swapped
  (Q641).
Rule: **translate in printed order and keep a story-order key in NOTES-TL.md** (Q641, high). Treat printed
order as presentation order and story order as the truth; add a note at the head of the file (Q253, high).
Check block order against content in every multi-block file (Q096). Never "improve" a near-duplicate
variant: diff each pair first (Q025, high).
Related: reading order across files is not chronological either — see §3.1.

### 1.13 Dakuten as a voice effect
A ゛ added to every kana of a line: 「と゛い゛う゛わ゛け゛で゛、本゛当゛に゛す゛み゛ま゛せ゛ん゛で゛し゛た゛……！」,
and separately to a bare ん: 「ん゛！？」. Two sites (00000BD6:11:46-47, 00000C68:11:101).
**Owner decision, high confidence a decision is needed** (Q176). Options recorded: (a) a typographic
mangling — doubled consonants, interpolated apostrophes; (b) a bracketed description; (c) small caps.
One device must cover both a whole sentence and a single syllable.

### 1.14 One kana / one syllable per cell
**~25 sites, the project's signature device.** Cell counts range from 3 to 17. Uses: a name being read
off a bottle 『ド／ロ／ー／ガ』 (Q004), a child calling for a parent 「お／と／う／さ／ん？」 (Q005), a dying
child's 「だ／い／す／き」 (Q182, owner), a suffocating narrator's 「さよ――／うな――／ら」 (Q130), a refusal
「お・／こ・／と・／わ・／り・／だ！」 (Q135), a seventeen-cell proverb 「登った階段、決して降りるべからず。」
(Q157), a phone breaking up 「あ――／こ――／先輩？」 (Q203), a comic pause 「い／ち／お／う」 (Q295), a
speech given back after four years (Q259), an intensifier repeated once per cell (Q072), a weight revealed
one digit at a time so the last cell holds two (Q272), an eight-digit code one digit per cell (Q650), a
sneeze built over four cells (Q759), a running joke split three ways with a different っ count each time
(Q842), 「よっ／こら／せっと！」 against a one-word glossary key (Q583), 「わ　　たし　を／こ／ろ／し／て」
(Q604), four hedges closed by an English word (Q613), an adverb in four spellings (Q903), syllables
separated by 「・」 AND split one per cell (Q084, Q115).
Rule: **keep the cell count and split the English at syllables or letters; letters may double up; do not
smooth** (Q004 medium, Q005 medium, Q157 high, Q259 high, Q272 medium, Q295 medium, Q604 low, Q759 high).
Where the JP puts the strongest word last, restructure the English so it still lands last (Q085, high).
Mark the interpunct beat with a hyphen ("SO-/ OR-/ ELSE-"), decided once for all instances (Q084, medium).
Pick one intensifier that survives repetition ("ultra") (Q072). One EN rule for all of them (Q135).
Open owner items in this family: Q182 (だいすき), Q650 (the backwards birthday), Q412/Q527 (see 1.15).

### 1.15 Repeated-character walls
A cell holding one syllable repeated dozens to hundreds of times, with no punctuation. Sites:
0000104E:11:38-52 — thirteen cells of one 「は」 then three cells of ~200 「は」 each (**Q412, owner**);
00000E7B:104:0-9 — 「殺したい」 x14 in one cell, then 7, 4, 2, 2, then 「殺し」, 「殺」, then three bare
「殺」 cells (Q234); 00000E7B:54:268-288 — 「ががが…」 at four distinct lengths alternating with
intelligible words in the same cell (Q240); 00000EF6:36:82 — 「死ねえええ！　なつみ！」 x8 in one cell, then
「なななつななつつつなななつつみみみなつつ――」 (Q335); 000008D0:46:145 — 「あっはは」 for ~60 syllables
(Q132); 00001325:8:200-215 — three unattributed 「キエエエ……！！」 cells, the first ~80 chars (**Q527,
owner**); 0000151C — a phrase repeated 3-6 times inside one cell, 「縊死縊死縊死縊死縊死縊死」 (Q622);
000010F5/00000E7B/000020F0 — three spellings of the same convulsive noise (Q474, Q850).
Rule: **keep the same syllable count where the box can hold it; reduce only where it cannot, and never
reduce to one** (Q622, medium). Strip intent, keep counts (Q335, high). A matching consonant run with the
lengths kept distinct, no italics (Q240, medium). Flag the long cells for the text-box measurement
together with Q049 (Q132, Q049). Q412 and Q527 must be decided together and by the owner.

### 1.16 Radio, broadcast, PA and press-conference text
Six sub-forms, all in one project:
- Station and drill announcements in straight double quotes (Q134).
- TV news in straight double quotes, interleaved one sentence at a time into a dying narrator's thoughts
  (Q238).
- Broadcast set as bare cells: a live report prefixed 「――」 interleaved into a phone call, five station
  announcements, a train guard's line in 「」, and a verdict report that OPENS a row-block before the
  narration (Q359).
- A studio host on 「――」 against a field reporter and a guest on 「」, in one block (Q414).
- Documentary narration on 「――」 returning after a quoted expert (Q690).
- Press conference: reporters' questions as bare 「――」 cells, the spokesman's answers in 「」, the radio
  anchor's link also in 「」 (Q656).
- A news broadcast in 「」 dropped into a file set fifteen years earlier with no date line and no frame
  (Q942).
Rule: treat prefixed 「――」 as broadcast, bare italic-less cells as announcements, and flag unattributed
voices individually (Q359, medium). Widen the straight-quote rule to cover announcements (Q134, high).
Keep the dash for documentary voice with no quotation marks (Q690, medium). Keep the prefix in the press
conference and let context do the work (Q656, medium).

### 1.17 Nested quotation
Up to **four levels deep** in one passage: 春花's narration → 五島 reading aloud in 「」 → the book's
old-style prose → 『』 around what a girl said 450 years ago (Q385). Other nests: a Nobel acceptance speech
of ~75 cells with a ~15-cell letter read aloud inside it (Q267); a narrator's account of a conversation
handed the first person INSIDE another first person, with the outer narrator's questions cut in as
ordinary 「」 so frame and inset share one passage (Q575); a sister's account handed the first person with
a third person's mutterings in 「」 inside it (Q582); a whole chapter-ending handover that never returns
(Q617); two inset first-person narrations with no frame at either end (Q400).
Rule: keep 『』 as single quotes, keep the seams unmarked exactly as the JP does, and let register do the
work — the inner voice is plainer and shorter (Q385 medium, Q400 high, Q575 medium, Q582 medium,
Q617 medium). See §3.2.

### 1.18 Timestamps, dated headers and captions
- Timestamp headers mixing a half-width and a full-width colon inside one file: 「6:00――」, 「15:00――」,
  「20：00――」 (Q341). Rule: normalise to one form (low).
- A dated caption in parentheses in no character's voice, opening a row-block: 「祀耀805年八月一日（元木駅
  集団発狂殺人事件から1年4か月後）」 (Q376). Rule: set it as a caption line, not as narration (high).
- A thriller caption in the Gregorian calendar in a project that dates in 祀耀: 「――時刻　2020年五月五日
  23:05」 followed by a countdown (**Q943, owner-blocking**; best guess 祀耀800 = 2020 CE).
- Dates in registry entries written with kanji numerals against STYLE's Arabic-digit rule (Q163). Rule:
  keep Arabic digits for readability but preserve the column spacing and the ⏎ breaks; flag for the font
  test (medium).
- Montage captions: three cells reading 「10分――」「20分――」「30分――。」, and four cells each ending
  「～を抜け――」 (Q594).
- Era-year welded onto an event name: 「大食祭804」 (Q270).
Ids: Q163, Q235, Q270, Q326, Q341, Q376, Q395, Q537, Q594, Q644, Q747, Q943.

### 1.19 Variables injected BETWEEN cells
The engine splices the player's name and a numeric variable between cells, so a cell ends mid-phrase and
the next begins with the honorific or the unit. 0000001E:4184:0 and 5044:0; 000000F2:759:0-1, 805:0-2 —
cells begin 「様、」 or 『 (Q007). 000015C2:8:0 is 『 alone and 8:1 is 様』でよろしいですか？;
0000198D:111:1 is a cell holding one full-width space (**Q683, owner-blocking**). 00001CA0:1059:0-1 is
「全体進行率」 then a cell beginning 「％……。」 (Q804).
Rule: keep "-sama," at the cell head and translate the fragments so they join naturally after a name plus
-sama; the honorific cannot be moved because it is in a separate cell; consider a TN (Q007 medium,
Q683 medium, Q804 high). Same handling for the numeric variable.

### 1.20 Full-width space as a device
Eight separate uses, all of which a cleanup pass would destroy:
1. **Gaps inside words**, standing for what a dying person stops being able to hear:
   「でも、茅萱ちゃんが女神様の弱点　気づいた　　ちょっと焦　　よ。」 (**Q555, owner-blocking** — keep the
   gaps as blank runs of proportional length, supply nothing).
2. **Gaps at different positions each time**, ~20 printings of one sentence across two files
   (**Q603, owner-blocking, LOW confidence**) and a converted corpse's speech (Q896 — same treatment as
   Q603, plus a TN).
3. **Voice floods**: unattributed fragments separated by full-width spaces inside single cells
   (00000488:48:0-12, Q050; 00000EDF:235:0-12, Q300 — several fragments are verbatim quotations of named
   characters from other files). Rule: render the space as a wide gap, no quotes, no periods, no
   attribution, no punctuation; match the earlier EN wording where the source line is identifiable
   (Q050 high, Q300 medium).
4. **A photograph with no text**, ten full-width spaces inside straight quotes (Q299 — keep ten, flag for
   the engine test).
5. **The core's turns**, printed nine times as a cell of eight full-width spaces inside quote marks
   (Q939).
6. **Cells holding one space and nothing else**: 000003AE:8:28 and 8:32 (Q046 — preserve one space, flag
   so no cleanup pass touches them); 00001039:19:1/3/6/8 (Q413 — keep them as empty cells, exactly as at
   000003AE).
7. **A whole file that is one cell holding a single full-width space**: 00001FE4 (Q769, owner — emit one
   full-width space, unchanged, high).
8. **Layout alignment**: leading spaces building a legend, growing indents 「マ／　ヨ／　醤／　油　！」,
   column spacing in registry extracts, line-internal gaps in a song's verses (Q721 owner-blocking,
   Q324 high, Q789 owner, Q534 medium).

### 1.21 Full-width katakana as a voice effect
Four sites, four causes. 「片言」 — a character answering in pure katakana, named as such by the text, then
two files later two other characters answering HER in the same register (Q073). 「スグニゲロ」 written in
blood and glossed by the narrator in the next cell (Q197). Two spoken cells reprinted as an unattributed
three-line block in full-width katakana (**Q497, owner-blocking**). A whole block of one speaker's lines in
full-width katakana with kanji kept, because chilli has destroyed his mouth (**Q735, owner-blocking**).
Rule where settled: for 片言, clipped article-less full-stop-separated speech ("I. Do. Not. Know.") and the
identical device in both files (Q073, medium). For the blood message, all-caps with no spacing
("RUNNOW"), keeping the next cell as the narrator working it out (Q197, high). Q497 and Q735 are owner
decisions; spaced small capitals is the least-bad candidate for Q497 and **italics are unavailable**
(CP932 / no italics), so the fallback is spacing or a TN.

### 1.22 Small kana, stretched っ, and slurred spelling
Small kana for a slurred mouth: 「ゃぁ……戦友……」, 「ゎたしはもぅ、だめだ……」 (Q104). Three small っ inside
a word: 「絶っっっ対に」 (Q510, TBD low); the same joke four times with a DIFFERENT っ count each time —
4, 6, 6, 5 (Q842). An old woman's greetings slurred in the spelling: 「あーら、いらっはーい」, 「はーい、
お待たせちゃーん」 (Q366). A mouth full of habanero decoding to an ordinary sentence (Q100). A drunk
slurring a name one cell after saying it correctly (Q809). A stammer on one mora from a man whose register
has never stammered (Q985).
Rule: render slurring with dropped letters and weak vowels rather than phonetic spelling; **"aye" and
"lass" are localisation and banned by STYLE.md** — carry archaic register with word order and verb form
(Q104, medium). Stretch a vowel in the EN intensifier and vary the letter count to match the っ count
(Q842, medium).

### 1.23 × / ○ / △ masking and deliberate blanks
- 「生まれて／××／年、茜と一緒よー！」 — an age blanked and split across three cells (Q023; rule: keep "XX",
  low).
- A whole file in which the patient's name and date of birth are ×××× and the phone line says 「××さん」
  (Q045; rule: keep unnamed, ungendered, and keep ×××× exactly, high).
- ××××, ○○○○ and △△△△ as censored body-part nouns, each alone in its own cell (Q141; rule: keep the
  symbols, the symbol counts and the cell splits, high).
- A note printed with runs of × for illegible characters, run lengths 1 to 6, ~20 consecutive cells, three
  of them reprinted verbatim in the next file and then printed again fully restored (**Q494,
  owner-blocking**; best guess: mask at matching WORD positions and keep masked cells identical between
  printings).
- 「私、／×／だけどいい？」 and 「××／の壁」 where × stands for a sex term (**Q832, owner**).

### 1.24 Latin script inside the Japanese
~15 sites. Portuguese liturgy lines, three at 00000A62 and three worse-formed at 00000DE9 that mix an
English noun phrase and a romanised Japanese verb into the Portuguese (Q162, Q195); two more Portuguese
lines spoken by a Westerner and never glossed (Q422); "Que droga" as a running gag that acquires a plot
function (Q178); English words in straight quotes glossed in the next cell and reused as the narrator's
private term (Q231); a film title (Q183); "Easy come, easy go." with the English full stop inside the cell
(**Q745, owner**); 「Maybe。」 closing four Japanese hedges (Q613); the 5W1H key with ruby residue inside
"How" (**Q467, owner**); 「英語のDrag」 where the intended word is "drug" (Q188, typo); four Portuguese cup
sizes whose fourth is actually English (Q834); the particle が typed as Latin "ga" (Q701, typo);
"Fascination", "helpless", "more helpless".
Rule: keep the Latin as printed; keep the on-screen gloss and add one [TN:] at first use saying the word is
in English/Portuguese in the source; do not delete the gloss (Q231, high). Keep the Latin word and either
vary the four preceding hedges in English or set the last rung in a different register, plus a TN
(Q613, medium). Romanisation of the Portuguese proper nouns is **Q378, owner-blocking**.

### 1.25 「・」 bullet lists
Five sites, all inside first-person narration or inside a quoted document: a four-item list of ability
conditions closed by 「だったはず。」 (Q250); three bullets inside a single spoken 「」 cell (Q305); a
five-characteristic list inside a quoted document inside first-person narration (Q539); a five-step plan
(Q552); a titled list — 「ドローガの注意点」 plus three 「・」 items — opening a row-block as bare cells
before any narration (Q661).
Rule: keep the bullets, keep the line breaks, keep the title cell and the items as separate cells
(Q305 low, Q661 medium).

### 1.26 Bare equation lines
Cells that are an equation, not a sentence: 「お父さん＝皮剥ぎ死体」 (Q022); a three-cell block
「井戸の死体＝サクラさんが首を切られた原因」 / 「息子＝主犯の1人」 / 「母親＝もう1人の主犯」 (Q202).
Rule: keep the equals sign and the cell split; do not turn them into sentences.

### 1.27 Kaomoji and text emotes
「ノ」 used as a raise-your-hand emote in board posts (Q018); a cell containing only (￣ー￣) (Q102);
(^-^) inside a quoted text message (Q471).
Rule: **leave the glyphs exactly as they are, verify them in the in-game font test, and do not replace them
with a western emoticon** (Q102, medium; Q471, medium). Note that (￣ー￣) uses U+FFE3, which is inside
CP932, so it survives; check each one against the character set before shipping.

### 1.28 Mojibake cells
Five navigator cells in 00002317 and 00002325 are CP932-rendered UTF-8 bytes, not Japanese. Decoded they
read ordinary navigator lines. Rule: **this is a dump artefact from the pylivemaker extraction, not source
text**; translate the decoded reading (Q872, high). Recorded decodes: 61:4 = 「てください」,
71:3 = 「この先のかなり複雑なルート分岐があるよう」, 37:3 = 「何が目的なのか。残りも少なくなってまいりましたが」,
47:0 = a near-duplicate of the 「編の途中で失礼致します」 formula.

### 1.29 Engine and UI instructions set as narration
「（次のページはクリックされるまで進行しません）」 printed as an ordinary narration cell in full-width
parentheses (Q164). The 事典 help text that warns items made of unreadable characters will appear (Q718).
Rule: translate as UI, keep the parentheses, do not give it the narrator's voice (Q164, low).

### 1.30 Kanji numerals against STYLE's Arabic-digit rule
「一五歳」 twice in a file that writes 「15歳」 elsewhere (Q235, typo); 「私の誕生日は四月三〇日」 (Q395);
「祀耀七七九年　五月一八日生」 in a register entry (Q163); the generation labels 第一世代（壱）, （弐）,
（参） in archaic numerals, then used as bare （壱）（弐）（参） for ~50 cells (Q537).
Rule: STYLE says Arabic digits for times, ages, counts. Keep Arabic in running text; keep the archaic
numerals as labels where they function as labels (Q537) and keep the column spacing in registry entries
(Q163).

### 1.31 Devices with no settled rule — the DECISION NEEDED list
Fifteen device-level items where the queries ask and never answer. All are in §2 as well; listed here so a
translator scanning §1 sees them:
| device | ids | status | best guess / confidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Two wrappers for documents vs phone messages | Q036, Q071, Q079 | open, high | distinct wrappers, wording unchosen |
| The 「――」 ordinary-secondary-speaker job | Q709 | owner-blocking, high | ordinary quotes + another split marker, OR keep the dash |
| Dakuten voice effect | Q176 | owner, high | one device covering a sentence and a single syllable |
| One kana per cell for a dying child | Q182 | owner, high | "I / love / you / so much" or reorder |
| Katakana voice effect (reprinted block) | Q497 | owner-blocking, medium | spaced small capitals, no italics |
| Katakana voice effect (burnt mouth) | Q735 | owner-blocking, high | small caps, flattened punctuation, or a TN |
| Full-width gaps at shifting positions x20 | Q603 | owner-blocking, LOW | gaps at matching positions, or a different device |
| × masking with matching run lengths | Q494 | owner-blocking, medium | mask at word positions, keep printings identical |
| Laughter as a layout object (~600 「は」) | Q412 | owner, medium | one "ha" per short cell, scale the long cells |
| Interior scream as ~80 chars | Q527 | owner, medium | must match the Q412 decision |
| Layout objects (legend, indents, ⏎ padding) | Q721 | owner-blocking, high | approximate the shape, note the impossibility |
| Generation tables typeset two ways | Q384 | owner-blocking, medium | keep ⏎ breaks, keep bare cells bare |
| The 『おひめの歌』 verse layout | Q789 | owner, high | verse form, full-width gaps as line-internal gaps |
| Grammatical-deletion hint | Q577 | owner-blocking, medium | EN fragments + renamed categories, or JP + TN |
| Stretched っ count | Q510, Q842 | open, low/medium | vowel stretch matched to the っ count |

Every QUERIES row with status `owner-blocking` (33) or `owner` (40). The orchestrator decides these;
a translator who hits one of these lines stops and asks rather than inventing. Format:
`id | file:line | question (short) | best guess`.

## 3. Recurring translation hazards and the fixed policy

### 3.1 Reading order is not chronological — and neither is block order
The whole work is a branching visual novel dumped into a flat file order. Reading order = lsb ID order =
navigator jump-table order = author creation order (PROGRESS head). It is NOT story order in either
direction. Confirmed jumps: 00000024 (order 2) is a prologue/flash-forward; orders 24-27 are a parallel
route; orders 38-39 precede orders 30-37; orders 40-46 are one continuous run; order 47 jumps two years;
order 48 rewinds to the day before everything; orders 61-71 rewind 12-20 years; order 132 is four years
later than the story present; 0000156A is set 祀耀815, fifteen years after the central event and ten years
further forward than anything else; the historical files run 800, 500 and 130 years back (Q024, Q054,
high). Block-level reordering is in §1.12.
Policy: every fact a translator uses carries an as-of file id, and the knowledge fence
(TRANSLATOR-BRIEF.md §2) applies. Never let a later file's answer leak into an earlier file's wording.

### 3.2 Unmarked narrator switches
**The single most common hazard in the project.** The script never labels a narrator. Switches happen at
four granularities:

**(a) At a file boundary, with no marker.** Dozens. 0000020B (Q030); 00000944/00000972 boundary (chunk 08);
0000154E (Q616 — the first proof is a speech tic at cell 26); 000021CF (Q919 — ends a 24-file run);
00002478 (Q983). Chunks 18-22 rotate three to four narrators across almost every file boundary
(PROGRESS 18, 19, 20, 21, 22).

**(b) Mid-file, at a row-block boundary.** 00002444 changes narrator at EVERY block boundary — 五島, 春花,
五島, 春花 — with no marker and no name for several cells at each seam (Q933, high). 00002450 loses its
narrator entirely at the [12]/[18] boundary: [18] is 217 cells of two-hander dialogue with no narration at
all (Q944, high).

**(c) Mid-block, inside one row-block.** The worst case. 000004BB:16:0-36 = なつみ, 16:37-62 = 五島 — the
switch happens on a phone call and the POV follows the call to the far end. 000004CF:12:0-59 = なつみ,
12:60-96 = 五島, 12:97-118 = なつみ. 00000617:8:0-42 = なつみ present, 8:43-252 = 五島 in a flashback two
years earlier, permanently (Q060, Q078, both high, boundaries recorded exactly in the rows).
00000E49:11:29-30 switches to 春花 and 11:117-118 switches back — the only seams are a line of dialogue
(「うん、あの時さ……」) and a closing clause (「とまあ、こんな感じだ。」) (Q220, high).
00000F0D has THREE narrators and no marker anywhere: 夏菜 for twelve blocks, 伊勢 for the whole of [56]
(signalled only by 俺 in its first sentence and by 「五島ちゃん」), なつみ for six cells inside [173] before
the voice passes back mid-block (Q350, high). 00001505:8:69 loses the frame to a second narrator for ~450
cells and never gives it back inside the file (Q605, medium). 0000156A has two mid-block seams: 143:32 and
193:76→193:114 (Q642, high).

**(d) No narrator at all.** 00000779's first 104 cells are third-person historic-present that names the
character and follows her senses, then switch to first-person past at 8:104 (Q108, medium).
00000E7B opens with twenty cells of radio procedure with no narrator. Whole files: 00001039 (Q413),
0000104E (Q433), 00001729 where nobody is named (Q692), 00002090/00002096 (Q833), 000020BB (Q835),
00002078 (Q836), 00002084 (Q844), 00001BA2/00001BA8 (Q801), 00001FE4 (Q769), 000021FE (Q888),
000023E7 where both speakers are unnamed (Q901).

**How a translator detects a switch — the only signals the JP gives:**
pronoun (私 / 俺 / 僕 / わたくし), speech level (です・ます vs plain), a speech tic (「ん……」, 「うぎゅ……」),
an address form used in narration (「古郡先輩」, 「チガ姉」, 「五島ちゃん」), tense, and content. Nothing else.

**Policy:** mark nothing in the English. Supply no name before the JP supplies it; do not gender-mark
before the JP does (Q448, Q198, Q108, all high). Let the register carry the seam (Q605, Q616, Q617, Q582).
**Record every seam in NOTES-TL.md before translating the file** (Q060, Q078, Q642, Q096, all high).
Where a file has a narrator who is never named, the CAST block and the address forms are the only
identification and the EN must reproduce that (Q640 高: 古郡茜 narrates 0000156A end to end and is never
named — the reader has three address forms and a sentencing formula; Q901, Q920, Q829, Q830).
Long single-narrator runs, for contrast: orders 75-85 (11 files), 117-126 (10), 156-163 (8), 178-185 (8),
0000212D-000021C9 (**24 consecutive files, no marker at either end and none at any of the 23 boundaries**,
Q828 high), 0000245F-00002473 (4 files, ~1,040 cells, Q964 high).

### 3.3 Inset first-person stories
A second character's first person is handed the narration inside the first narrator's first person, with
**no frame at either end**. At least twelve sites.
- Four ghost stories told in unquoted first person inside 古郡なつみ's first person, 60-130 cells each,
  returning to 「」 at the end (Q146, high).
- The 肉付きの面 folktale, ~42 cells, then used three more times by the narrator about herself (Q322).
- 00000F24:64:66-85 and 187:11-37 — two insets, one narrating an abduction, one a stairwell (Q400, high).
- 00001459:8:199-237 — the inset and the frame share one passage, with the outer narrator's questions cut
  in as ordinary 「」 (Q575, medium).
- 0000148E:8:231-247 — handed in and handed back the same way, with a third voice in 「」 inside it
  (Q582, medium).
- 0000154E:8:139-228 — **the chunk ENDS inside the inset**; ninety cells with no return (Q617, medium).
- 0000156A:193:76-113 — 38 cells (Q642).
- 00002450:12:39-50 — a three-year-old's words quoted inside her elder sister's recollection, alternating
  the child and the adult twelve years apart with no frame marking whose cells are whose (Q945).
- The anonymising device inside a told legend: 「その子を仮にA子とします」, used ~20 times (Q159; rule:
  "Girl A" throughout, with the introducing sentence carrying the explanation, medium).
Policy: **mark the frame in the notes, not in the text.** Keep the JP's lack of quotation marks, hold the
inset in the same plain past, and let the register shift be the only seam (Q146 high, Q400 high,
Q575 medium, Q582 medium, Q617 medium).

### 3.4 Precognition, hallucination, dreams and retracted passages
Long stretches of ordinary-looking narration turn out not to have happened. **Nothing inside them may be
used to settle an earlier query, and nothing in the EN may hedge them.**
| site | what it is | ids |
|---|---|---|
| 00000EDF (~2,300 cells) | four in-story days, a funeral, two murders — all one instant of borrowed precognition, retracted at 117:162-169 | Q285 (high) |
| 00000EF6 blocks 140/134/110/67 + 61 replay | a comatose character speaks in her own exact register; the reveal is printed THIRTEEN blocks before the scenes it retracts | Q318, Q319, Q333 (high) |
| 00000F0D:44:187-242 | a ~55-cell precognitive vision; the only markers are 「――！！」 and a return sentence afterwards | Q355 (high) |
| 00000F0D:44:66-71 | four 「」 cells nobody says, introduced by 「本能が私に告げる――」 | Q356 (high) |
| 00000F57:11:96-131 | a 36-cell arrest scene retracted at 11:131 with one line | Q409 (high) |
| 00001385:8:0-17 | seventeen cells at full pitch, retracted in one cell: 「――と、ここで私の目が覚めたのだった」 | Q546 (high) |
| 000014BC:8:183-202 | a fifteen-cell vision; the only markers are a door blowing open before and a gasp after | Q588 (high) |
| 000010F5:8:0-91 | 88 cells revealed as a dream only at 8:88-91 | Q453 (high) |
| 0000110D:8:16-24:119 | a whole memory with no frame; the return is a single 「はあ……」 mirroring the opening one | Q457 |
| 00001EFF:8:21-42 | ~22 cells named as 白昼夢 afterwards and then renamed a nine-year-old memory | Q805 (high) |
| 00002444:26:306-355 | ~48 cells of dialogue that never happened, in 「」 and 「――」 exactly like a real scene | Q937 (high) |
| 00000697:8:115-121 | seven cells of dialogue with an absent character; the text says so only at 8:122 | Q095 (medium) |
| 00001442:11:192-317 | a whole gentle scene retracted two files later as manipulation | Q574 |
| 000010F5 / 00001154 / 00001184 | a confession to five murders, and its forensic refutation, both true-seeming and both planted | Q460, Q461, Q462 (all resolved, see §5) |
Policy: **translate everything straight. Add no hedges, no foreshadowing, no tense signals, no italics.**
The only cell that may know is the retracting cell (Q285, high). Do not signal a dream early and do not add
a scene break (Q453, high). Play the retracted cells at full pitch; the retraction is one cell and must not
be softened by hindsight elsewhere in the file (Q546, high). The hallucinated version of a character has
her **own CAST block**; do not hedge her lines (Q318, high). A narration that asserts a miracle the reader
has already been shown to be a hallucination is never corrected — leave it uncorrected (Q333).

### 3.7 Aspect, tense and grammar distinctions that carry plot
English cannot reproduce these by grammar; the notes require them to be carried by word choice and word
order, with the surrounding reasoning left intact.
- **死んでいる vs 死んだ** (00000ADE:11:82-97, 11:161-164). A whole branch's clue and the narrator's death
  turn on the ている state form, and on 確かに normally preceding an adversative. She reasons about the
  grammar out loud for fifteen cells and repeats it verbatim. Rule: the EN must make "is dead" sound like a
  statement about a body in front of the speaker rather than about a past event, and must let "certainly"
  sound like the first half of "certainly..., but". **Both passages must be word-identical** (Q168, high).
- **A contextually wrong 「でも」** (00001477:8:111-131, 8:158). The entire clue is one adversative that
  contradicts nothing; the narrator names it (「逆接を用いるべき会話ではないはず」) and reconstructs the
  deleted middle from it. Rule: keep "but" in the same position and let the narration's naming of it do the
  work (Q578, high).
- **A negation pair** (00000DB9:11:104-105). Two cells identical except for one negation. Rule:
  byte-identical EN except "innocent" / "not innocent" (Q208, high).
- **Five sentences identical but for the crime and the interval, the fifth inverted on 消えて『いなかった』
  with the negation inside 『』** (00000DA1:11:175-181). Rule: five byte-identical EN sentences varying only
  in the named crime and the year count, with the negation in single quotes (Q201, high).
- **A sentence split so the first half states and the second withdraws** (00000E19:11:43-44), the second
  cell opening on a bare 「のかどうか」. Rule from the row: "—she doesn't look like she's thinking anything
  in particular... / Or does she. I can't tell." (Q227, medium).
- **One verb used three times, twice as a quoted phrase and once as the sentence's own verb**
  (000014BC:8:107-108: 『何かが引っ掛かる』の逆で、『何も引っ掛からない』から引っ掛かるのだ). Rule: find one EN
  verb that works both ways and keep the single quotes on the two quoted forms (Q587, medium).
- **One adverb in four spellings across four cells** — kana, kanji, and a second kanji meaning fast-moving
  rather than soon (0000241B:11:28-31). Rule: four cells of the same English word with rising punctuation
  plus a TN (Q903, high).
- **「知らない」 chosen over 「覚えていない」/「忘れた」**, and the narration flags the choice
  (0000246E:11:323-333) (Q969).
- **A question punctuated as a question and read by everyone as a confession** (000014EE:16:324) (Q639).
- **A hint built from deleted subject, predicate and modifier**, with the three grammatical categories
  named on screen (**Q577, owner-blocking**) — Japanese stays grammatical with all three gone, English does
  not.
- **Honorific-target error as characterisation**: 尊敬語 applied to one's own side, flagged by the narration
  as 「妙な敬語」 (Q136); 「美冬、金井さん！」 with the narration 「名と姓を逆に呼ばれ」 (Q117).
- **The pronoun switch 僕 → 俺 for exactly fourteen cells while a drug is active**, back with no comment
  (Q447); 僕 twice and 俺 three cells later in one conversation (Q995). STYLE.md: never re-encode a pronoun
  by warping English grammar — translate plainly and add a [TN:] at the line.

### 3.9 Dialect
STYLE.md has **no dialect policy**. Five sites, two of them sustained characterisation:
- 関西弁 throughout one character's file and beyond — やねん, へん, んよな, ほんま, せや, ええ, しんどい,
  ほな (**Q611, owner-blocking, LOW**). Best guess: **no British or American regional dialect**; carry it
  with rhythm, blunt word choice and a few fixed tags, and write the rule into STYLE.md before any file
  with her in it is translated.
- Sustained northern dialect from two grandparents — じゃじゃじゃ, ごの, ～だ, ～が, 桜っこ, んが, じっちゃん
  (**Q785, owner-blocking**, high). One policy for both, decided before either file is drafted.
- An Italian TV chef who speaks fluent 津軽弁 and does comic patter with it (Q070, medium). Rule: **name
  the dialect** ("fluent Tsugaru dialect") and add a short TN; do not assign an English accent.
- A liar caught by his 飯沢県 accent whose lines are NOT written in dialect anywhere
  (**Q843, owner-blocking**, medium). Rule: mark it in the narration only, as the JP does; do NOT retrofit
  an accent onto his dialogue.
- The ～ざます／～ですわ stereotyped rich-woman register (Q074, medium). Rule: over-elaborate genteel English
  (no contractions, "my dear", "positively") rather than a regional accent; do not make them sound old.
- The ～アル pseudo-Chinese register from a background waiter (Q149, high). Rule: do **NOT** use broken
  "Engrish"; owner decision needed.
Related register hazards that are NOT dialect: 五島's three comic tiers — the ゲス／旦那／御意 pastiche
(Q019), the ～っす slackening that recurs four times (Q094, Q304, Q501), and the written samurai-retainer
humble register 小生／御尊父様／五臓六腑 (Q177); the プリマベラ 最上級敬語 used as a weapon (Q707); 御令姪,
最上級敬語 for another person's niece (**Q479, owner**).

### 3.10 Source documents quoted in more than one typographic system
The same kind of object is quoted four different ways, sometimes in one file. A research report in 『』 one
paragraph per cell for ~20 cells against a scripture as bare cells with no marking for ~45 cells, in two
files (Q504); a 400-year-old passage in straight double quotes four cells after a bare-cell scripture
(Q503); a teaching recalled from memory with no marks at all, framed only by 「要所要所を思い出してみると
――」 (Q560); the ancestral transmission in six printings across three systems (Q567, Q619, Q841).
Policy: **keep the treatments distinct rather than harmonising them** (Q504, medium). Q560 must read as
remembered rather than quoted — keep it unmarked and let the register shift carry it (medium).

### 3.11 Things stated on screen that a translator must not "fix"
- Contradictions the author left in and, in one case, admitted to: dates that disagree
  (Q112, Q387, Q514, Q515, Q670, Q827), a 三回忌 that is arithmetically right in one file and wrong in
  another (Q194 vs Q112), two names for one legal status (Q399), two names for one building (Q184), two
  names for one massacre (Q647, owner-blocking), two names for one earthquake (Q822), 「第3回元木町春の
  大食い大会」 vs 「第3回元木大食い祭り」 with the narrator flagging it herself (Q723), a methamphetamine /
  ephedrine relationship inverted against every other file (Q415), a covering note dated two different ways
  (Q827, owner-blocking), and the author's own statement of an unresolved plot contradiction in the
  afterword, broken off mid-sentence (Q876).
- On-screen unit conversions the file then reasons with for 2,000 lines (Q273, owner: keep every conversion
  cell as written).
- Sentences that break off and are never completed: Q126, Q685, Q698, Q718, Q750, Q778, Q803, Q858, Q860,
  Q952. Keep every one broken off.
- Questions asked and answered by the narration itself as a written-in branch point (Q097).
- Real-world material used as plot: a real 1938 mass killing named as the narrator's precedent (Q239), the
  Oregon honey-fungus mycelium and a Dutch particle-copying claim (Q665), a real Tokyo river (Q473), a
  statute cited by article number (Q365), 両墓制 (Q338), 稀血 / シスAB型 / ボンベイ型 (Q328), 村八分 (Q800),
  等活地獄 (Q364), 永劫回帰 (Q889), ブロークンウィンドウ理論 given in both katakana and calque in one cell
  (Q948, Q963).
- Numbers and arithmetic done on screen: 「確率はまだ12分の10」, 「11分の5」, Scoville values, 「1兆6653億年」,
  「12ダース － 1」 with a spaced half-width minus sign, 0.03%, 10210 revealed one digit at a time
  (Q099, Q248, Q272, Q331, Q395).

## 4. Source-typo policy and the typo id list

**Policy (STYLE.md, Fable 2026-09-24, after chunk 08):** source typos — dropped or doubled kana, obvious
IME misconversions, wrong particles, punctuation slips — are **translated as the intended text. Do NOT
reproduce the typo in English.** Each one is logged in QUERIES.md with the exact JP and status=typo. There
is no "sic", no deliberate misspelling, no bracket.

**Exception to watch for:** deliberate broken Japanese is NOT a typo and must be carried. The cases that
look like typos and are not:
- 谷崎's school email is written in broken Japanese **on purpose** — 「とのことだす」 for 「とのことです」,
  詳しい／詳しく in one clause, 「よい休日を！」 closing a notice about a murder — and the narration names the
  habit at 8:51 (Q088, open, not typo).
- A child's forged diary written in a ten-year-old's hand: 交かん日記 for 交換日記, ひなだん for ひな壇, no
  kanji above school level (Q525, open).
- Baby-speech spellings quoted verbatim twenty years later (Q411).
- An old woman's greetings slurred in the spelling (Q366).
- Small-kana slurring and the 片言 katakana register (Q104, Q073).
When in doubt, check whether the narration comments on it. If a character or the narrator notices the
oddity, it is a device; if nobody notices, it is a typo.

**What the 124 typo rows actually contain** (four kinds, roughly):
1. Dropped, doubled or wrong kana and particles (~85): 行かななければ, 何としかして, なるんて, 亡くなっし,
   落ちつている, 知られた〈ら〉, 妹だった〈ら〉, 分かんない〈ん〉だけど, 私しかない for 私しかいない,
   髪の気 for 髪の毛, どころで for ところで, 望んていた, などど, おかげて, 撮って for 撮ってる,
   を where が is required (Q719, Q758, Q994), etc.
2. Kanji and name misconversions (~15): ドンキ for 鈍器, 元の子もない for 元も子もない, 森と for 森閑と,
   宗綱 for 綱宗 (reversed, in a file that writes it correctly eleven times), 今川 for 間川, 勅旨 for 令旨,
   良作 for 良策, 始めて for 初めて, 証明証 for 証明書, レポドパ for レボドパ, 800度 for 800年度,
   ポンチョブラケット for ポンチョブランケット, クライスメイト for クラスメイト, 「英語のDrag」 for "drug".
3. Punctuation and character-width slips (~15): a cell that opens 「 and never closes it (Q212, Q284); a
   doubled closing 」 (Q909/Q922); a full stop after ？ or ！ (Q403, Q408, Q486); two 句点 (Q523); a
   half-width ideographic 「｡」 (Q435); a half-width space before a full-width ？ (Q956); half-width
   brackets round a numeral where the file uses full-width (Q538); half-width vs full-width colon mixed in
   one file (Q341, Q695); a trailing full-width space (Q348); cells missing the terminal 。 where every
   neighbour has one (Q439, Q681, Q848, Q992); okurigana inconsistency (Q815); 様 vs さま in one speech by
   one speaker (Q446); the particle が typed as Latin "ga" (Q701); 一五歳 in kanji where the file writes
   15歳 (Q235).
4. Duplicates kept deliberately for cross-referencing from a SUMMARY block: Q922 duplicates Q909, Q923
   duplicates Q911.

**The 124 typo ids:**

## 5. Resolved rows whose resolution changes a rendering

Twelve rows. Each is a question a translator of the EARLIER file would otherwise get wrong. The knowledge
fence still applies: the answer is known to the translator, and must not appear in the earlier file's text.

| id | file:line | resolution, and what it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Q003 | 00000024:47:17-21 vs 000001E3:8:150-152 | The "posted overseas three years ago" account is a **lie told by the character herself**; the father died twelve years ago on 四月八日 falling while retrieving a balloon. Told twice, in two branches, in two registers. Resolved by 000004E3:27:117; 00000550:11:42-44; 000005AC:11:78-86. The earlier EN must sound like a lie being told smoothly, not like exposition — and must NOT foreclose the truth. The man at 00000024:51:42 is NOT thereby explained. |
| Q006 | 000000F2 whole file | The menu navigator (わたくし, top keigo) **gets a CAST block**. Resolved by a brief addition (Fable). Changes: her register is a character voice to be kept consistent across ~25 navigator files, not UI boilerplate. |
| Q016 | 000001E1:33:10 – 000001E5:23:26 | The dream figure's sex is explicitly unknowable in the span; resolved at 00000641:11:25-37 as a named male character. **The EN constraint for the earlier span is UNCHANGED: he must not be gendered before 000001E5:23:26.** The translator knows the answer and must not write it. |
| Q035 | 00000024:51:11 | 「伊勢さん」 = 元木警察署警部補 伊勢大二郎. Resolved by 000003B4:8:14; 000004A7:11:41. Fixes the rank vocabulary (警部補 / 巡査部長 / 警部補殿, see Q055) from the first mention. |
| Q051 | 00000488:48:7; 00000550:11:67-70 | 荒田集落 is a **place in the story present** — 荒田地区 / 通称荒田集落, in 飯沢県, mountains, few people, many unusual customs, one family's home, the source of the 十年祭 reckoning. The **reading あらた remains unconfirmed**, and the kanji pun on it is owner-blocking (Q418). |
| Q105 | 00000697:8:46; 000006E0:16:87 | School years fixed: 春花 > なつみ > 五島, one year apart each (3年1組, 2年2組, 1年). **Fix the "one year older" wording wherever it appears.** Complicated later by 「18年間」 in an epilogue (Q113). |
| Q153 | 0000092D:11:98-101; 000009CF:11:42-44 | The head scar: she took the injury shielding the other girl ten years ago, told to the narrator by her own parents; **she herself still does not remember it**. Resolved by 00000E95:11:110, 11:158-161. The amnesia stays as written and the EN must stay as blank as the JP — **do not add a single connecting word**. |
| Q155 | 000009E6:19:12-15 | 『モトキザクラ様』 — keep the 様, render "Motoki-zakura-sama". Resolved by 000009FE:11:0-43:13, where the story is told in full. Later given an invented origin in an occult magazine (Q648). |
| Q216 | 00000E19:11:183; 00000E31:11:44-47 | The adoption is real and is narrated on screen by both parents at 00000E95:11:81-88, 11:147, 11:157; both girls say 姉妹 out loud and she gives her surname as 古郡 at 11:122. Fixed EN recorded in the row: "Haruka-chan is adopted. She's really a daughter of the Kogori house — Natsumi-chan's real older sister." **Whether she is being adopted INTO the house or returning to it is still open.** Affects Q246, Q410 (the 「お父さん、お母さん」 attempts) and Q217 (owner: お姉ちゃん vs お姉さん). |
| Q460 | 000010F5:8:348-357; 0000110D:8:25 | She **never injected herself.** The thing gave her the sight and sound of doing it where nobody could witness it, and her own honesty made the belief stick. Resolved by 000014D7:8:256-266. **000010F5 must be translated as what she believed she was doing** — no hedge, no irony, and do not make the link the next file refuses to make (Q460's own rule: "do not make the link in EN"). |
| Q461 | 00001154:8:36-53 | The five confessed murders, one per April, ages 11-15, dated 祀耀791-795, are **planted visions**, stated as such by another character as a corpse at 000014D7:8:270. Rule stands: **translate flat, no dramatic diction.** |
| Q462 | 00001184:20:0, 24:0-24:26 | The forensic contradiction that retracts Q461 nine cells later (every corpse beaten, not stabbed) **stands, because she never did it**. Resolved by the same cell, 000014D7:8:270. Rule: keep the one-cell block alone with nothing added. |

**Reading of the set:** four of the twelve resolutions (Q003, Q016, Q460, Q461, Q462) resolve to "the
earlier text is a lie or a planted vision, and the English must not know that." That is the dominant shape
of this project's resolved rows and the dominant risk in translating it: the temptation to add a hedge.
Do not.

# PART C — MAP, COLLISIONS, NAME INDEX
## 1. Branch and time map

### 1.0 How to read this map

- Reading order = lsb ID order = the navigator's jump-table order = author creation order.
  It is NOT story order and (after the first two scenarios) NOT play order.
  Verified in game (notes/PROGRESS.md §"Section 1"): the first scenario offered is 00000024 and
  the next is 000001DB, so lsb order = play order at the start only.
- The scenario-database title -> lsb mapping is UNRESOLVED (PROGRESS.md, top). So the 編 (volume)
  names below are attached to files ONLY where a navigator file says so on screen. Do not infer a
  file's 編 from anything else.
- The 編 the game itself declares (notes/_db/編.tsv, ID order, with its point multiplier):
  0 呪殺編 (1.75), 1 明徴編 (2), 2 詫言編 (2), 3 死月編 (2.5), 4 最後の声編 (2.5),
  5 真・呪殺編 (5), 6 真・明徴編 (50), 7 エデンの桜編 (5).
  notes/_db/ルート概要.tsv adds a 永劫回帰編 that 編.tsv does not list.
  Navigator files close them in this reading order: 呪殺編 at order 234 (0000185D);
  明徴編 at order 241 (0000198D); 明徴編+詫言編 at order 262 (00001AF3, which then announces
  第四編『死月編』); 死月編 at order 291 (00001C43); a FALSE ending at orders 295-297;
  真・呪殺編 endings at orders 306-311; 真・明徴編 at order 313 (00001E47);
  ボツシナリオ (the cut route) announced at order 391 (000021D5).
- In-story calendar: the fictional era is 祀耀. 祀耀800年四月八日 is the project's centre.
  Two pegs exist and they do not come from the story proper:
  order 203 (000015C2:48:4-48:5) has the navigator say 「祀耀800年が…西暦2020年頃」 (-> Q014), and
  order 416 (00002450:18) prints a caption 「――時刻　2020年五月五日　23:05」 (-> Q943, owner-blocking).
  Everything else in 422 files is dated in 祀耀 only.
- Column order below: order range | file ids | narrator(s) | in-story time frame | how it ends |
  what it rewinds to or branches from.

- **418-421 | 0000245F..00002473 | 新村幸太郎 (俺), FOUR consecutive files, ~1,040 cells with no marker at any boundary — his longest narration by a factor of ten, where every earlier stretch was under 100 cells (-> Q964). 00002464 opens by reprinting the previous file's last two cells verbatim (-> Q965) | the morning, afternoon and evening of the 十三回忌, 祀耀800年四月八日, with inset flashbacks to 祀耀788, 祀耀790 and 祀耀793 | he is pulled out from under the tree screaming and taken away swearing revenge before he rots | THE CLIMAX FROM THE ANTAGONIST'S INSIDE. Order 421 covers the SAME THIRTY MINUTES as 00000E19 and 00000E49 (orders 128, 130) from the other side, with ~15 spoken cells word-identical (-> Q973, Q025).**
- **422 | 00002478 | 新村茅萱 (私), taken at the file boundary with no marker; the 「ん……」 tic on every spoken line and absent from narration | weeks after the 十三回忌, then 「何日も、何か月も」, then 祀耀801年四月八日 | THE LAST FILE OF THE PROJECT: 春花 wakes on 祀耀801年四月八日 and 「また新しく、そして大きく事態が動き出した。／のだが――／それはまた――別の世界での話。」, which hands the whole work back to the navigator frame of 00001CA0 and 000021E2 (-> Q987, Q763, Q884).**

### 1.2 Structural hazards a translator of any file must expect

Derived from the runs above; the per-file detail is in the SUMMARY block.

- **Unmarked narrator seams at file boundaries.** The commonest seam in the project. Orders where it happens: 10/11, 98/99, 100-104 (every boundary), 130/131, 140-146 (almost every boundary), 163/164 and six more seams inside 164-177, 185/186, 189/190, 191/192, 196/197, 200/201, 365/366, 389/390, 412/413, 414/415, 417/418, 421/422. -> Q030, Q078.
- **Unmarked narrator seams INSIDE a row-block.** Orders 28, 29, 44 (00000617:8:40), 59 (00000779:8:104), 92, 94, 100, 130 (switches and switches back), 138, 139, 198 (00001505:8:69, ~450 cells and never switches back), 201 (0000154E:8:139), 202 (143:32 and 193:76-193:113). -> Q060, Q078, Q400.
- **Row-blocks printed out of scene order.** Orders 70, 72, 76, 134 (near-reverse), 136 (shuffled), 137 (reverse except one forward run), 138, 139 (ascending block number = non-story order), 146, 202 (nineteen blocks, eleven of them in strict reverse). -> Q096, Q253, Q317, Q380, Q641.
- **Verbatim replays across files.** The EN must be word-identical on both sides. Orders 49/48, 96/90, 133/131, 186/185, 192/174, 193 (fourth telling), 199/198, 415, 419/418, 421/128+130. -> Q025, Q557, Q580, Q581, Q965, Q973.
- **Retractions.** Order 136 retracts ~2,300 cells as borrowed precognition (-> Q285); order 137 retracts four blocks as hallucination (-> Q318); order 155's self-injection and order 159's five-murder confession are both retracted at order 196 as planted hallucinations (-> Q460, Q461, Q462, resolved); order 409 and order 546-era capture scenes are retracted as dreams (-> Q409, Q546).
- **Files with no narrator at all.** Orders 1, 3, 20-23, 114, 147, 149, and the great majority of orders 203-266 and 332-365, plus 396-399, 401-406.
- **Pre-modern register.** Orders 150-153 and the inset at order 198 (糸姫's 800-year-old memory) and orders 366-389 (新村桔梗, 122 years back). These need a distinct English register from the modern files.

## 3. EN-collision list

`cut -f3 notes/GLOSSARY.tsv | sort | uniq -d` over 3,110 rows returns SEVENTEEN duplicated English
renderings. PROGRESS.md records that chunks 19-28 resolved a further ~21 collisions in the note
column by choosing distinct renderings, so these seventeen are what is left.

Each group below gives the shared EN, the jp keys that share it, the first_seen of each, and a
verdict taken from the note column: **same-referent** (one thing, two spellings or a source typo —
one EN is correct) or **en-collision** (two different things — one of the two EN renderings must
change before either file is translated).

1. **"a revelation"** — **en-collision.**
   - 預言 (よげん, term, 00000656:11:180) — note: the 預 spelling, not 予言; keep it distinct from なつみ's 予知能力.
   - お告げ (おつげ, term, 00000D29:19:7) — note: the 荒田 word for the 魔女 naming the 呪殺 culprit; argued about across four files, so one fixed EN word is required.
   - Both are load-bearing terms in different registers. 預言 is scriptural (the 女神様's 預言 about 春花ちゃん), お告げ is the settlement's working word. They cannot share one EN.

2. **"a revolt"** — **en-collision.**
   - 一揆 (いっき, term, 00000EAF:28:291) — modern, used figuratively.
   - 謀反 (むほん, term, 000010C3:11:10) — pre-modern, 祀耀340, a court rebellion.
   - Different eras, different registers; no note on either row.

3. **"a sleeping draught"** — **en-collision**, already marked as such in the source.
   - 導眠剤 (どうみんざい, object, 00000EF6:61:222) — modern pharmaceutical.
   - 眠り薬 (ねむりぐすり, object, 000010C3:11:218) — note: `en-collision: 導眠剤`. Pre-modern.

4. **"a torch"** — **en-collision.** Two different objects.
   - たいまつ (object, 00000E7B:180:45) — a burning brand.
   - 懐中電灯 (かいちゅうでんとう, object, 00000EDF:192:27) — an electric torch. In EN these must be "a torch/brand" vs "a flashlight/torch"; pick one and move the other.

5. **"an outsider"** — **en-collision.**
   - 部外者 (ぶがいしゃ, term, 00000B0F:11:140) — note: the settlement's category for anyone who does not die in April.
   - 異邦人 (いほうじん, term, 00000F24:100:98) — note: used of the Westerners in the 450-year-old account.

6. **"blunt weapon"** — **same-referent.**
   - 鈍器 (どんき, object, 0000020D:11:90).
   - ドンキ (object, 00000B27:11:113) — note: apparent source typo for 鈍器 -> Q172. Per STYLE.md the intended text is translated and the typo is not reproduced, so both cells get the same EN and the typo is logged.

7. **"Heh"** — **en-collision.**
   - あは (interjection, 000001DB:15:38) — note: なつみ's short laugh.
   - えへ (interjection, 00000EC7:11:71) — note: `en-collision: えへへ already holds the longer form`.

8. **"Hey"** — **en-collision.**
   - ねえ (interjection, 00000024:16:27) — note: soft vocative, なつみ's.
   - よう (interjection, 000001DB:15:28) — note: 春花's greeting; must read rougher than ねえ. 春花's 「よう！　調子はどうだ？」 is a FIXED greeting established at 000001DB, so this one is the one that must not move.

9. **"Hm"** — **en-collision.**
   - ん (interjection, 000001E7:11:52) — and note that 「ん……」 is also 新村茅萱's fixed line-opening tic, which is a separate job for the same kana.
   - ふうん (interjection, 000002F6:11:29) — note: non-committal acknowledgement.

10. **"Hmm"** — **en-collision.**
    - ふむ (interjection, 00000460:11:21) — note: 五島's thinking noise; ふむーん and ふーむ are the same sound stretched.
    - うーん (interjection, 00000F24:94:186) — note: deliberating; distinct from ふうん (00000779).

11. **"Hmmm"** — **mixed: one same-referent, one en-collision.**
    - ふーむ (interjection, 00000EC7:11:164) — note: `en-collision: ふむ already holds "Hmm"`. But the ふむ row itself says ふーむ is the SAME sound stretched, so ふーむ vs ふむ is **same-referent** and the EN should be the stretched form of whatever ふむ gets.
    - ほーん (interjection, 00000F0D:38:149) — note: 五島絵梨奈, once, before she starts probing. A different sound: **en-collision** with the ふむ family.

12. **"mumbling"** — **en-collision.**
    - ぼそぼそ (sfx/adverb, 00000538:11:35) — note: 伊勢 at the mortuary; also 00000522:11:17.
    - もごもご (sfx/adverb, 000005C1:11:222) — note: 春花 evading; must differ from 伊勢's ぼそぼそ.

13. **"Nnngh"** — **en-collision.**
    - ぎー (interjection, 00000564:11:27) — note: 春花's frustrated squeal.
    - ぐぬぬ (interjection, 000005C1:11:280) — note: losing an argument; 春花 here, なつみ at 00000600:19:43.

14. **"shuddering"** — **en-collision**, and part of a larger family.
    - ぶるぶる (sfx/adverb, 00000E7B:68:158) — note: `en-collision: ガクガク "shaking", わなわな "trembling", がたがた "rattling"`.
    - びくびく (sfx/adverb, 00000EF6:61:165).

15. **"swaying"** — **en-collision.**
    - ゆらゆら (term/adverb, 00000ADE:11:118) — inside the narrator's own death narration, so it is load-bearing.
    - ふらふら (sfx/adverb, 00000F24:88:192).

16. **"Tch"** — **en-collision.**
    - ちっ (interjection, 000006F9:17:57) — note: 五島's tongue click; the narration calls it 舌打ち, so the EN must be readable as a tongue click.
    - ちぇ (interjection, 00000EF6:8:215) — note: 新村春花's, in her last line of the branch.

17. **"the Shirinkan"** — **same-referent, and the collision is the POINT.** Do not separate them.
    - 詩林館 (しりんかん, place, 00000A18:11:72) — the building's real name.
    - 死隣館 (しりんかん, place, 00000A18:11:164) — note: written on a board; same reading as 詩林館, "death-neighbour house". The ghost story's whole mechanism is that the two are homophones -> **Q160 (owner)**. The EN must keep one sound with two spellings, or add a TN; a rendering that makes them look different defeats the file.

---

## 4. Name index

Every JP name form that appears for a person, mapped to the ONE EN rendering that form gets,
with the as-of id where a form starts or changes. Built from the 108 `person` rows and the 34
`address` rows of notes/GLOSSARY.tsv, the 167 CAST.md block headings, and the `to` + `calls_them`
columns of notes/RELATIONS.tsv (762 rows). Who says which form to whom is GLOBAL-A's address
matrix; this section is only the FORM -> EN mapping.

STYLE.md rules applied throughout: Western order (given name first); honorifics preserved as
suffixes (-san, -kun, -chan, -senpai, -sensei, -sama, -dono, -nee, -nii); kin suffixes ride the
name (チガ姉 -> Chiga-nee); standalone kinship words are translated by register (お母さん -> Mom,
おばさん -> Auntie, お姉ちゃん -> Sis (P)); modified Hepburn, NO macrons, long vowels dropped;
historical names with の and bare titles stay as they are.

### 4.0 Four unresolved readings where GLOSSARY and CAST.md DISAGREE

These are not style choices. The two notes files give different readings for the same kanji and
one of the two must be wrong. Do not translate a file containing these names until the orchestrator
picks. (Reported as an open problem.)

| kanji | GLOSSARY.tsv says | CAST.md heading says | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 城崎 (健吾) | しろざき / **Kengo Shirozaki** ("reading しろざき per cast list") | — | the surname of 城崎健吾, a major speaker from 00000B27 on |
| 城崎 (百合子) | きざき / **Yuriko Kizaki** (person row, 00001154:8:18) | きざき, Yuriko Kizaki | SAME surname, different reading from her husband's. One of the two is wrong. |
| 後白河糸織 | ごしらかわ **いおり** / Iori Goshirakawa ("reading いおり per cast list") | ごしらかわ **いとおり** / Itoori Goshirakawa | the transfer student of 00001533; 糸織 is built on 糸 (糸姫), which favours いとおり |
| 新村晃 | にいむら **こう** / Ko Niimura ("reading unconfirmed -> Q595") | にいむら **あきら** / Akira Niimura | 新村桔梗's husband, present in all 24 files of orders 366-389 |
